Q10Ingest the failure/activity email corpus (.msg + Copilot JSON) with an SME surface for assigning an email to a folder or process
drydocs-docmetaP14requirementp2fable / main
docmeta
depends on: G31

Acceptance: A doc-source-registry entry declares the email corpus with its classification, connector: email, tier, curation ladder, target_db and retention note; the corpus loads as the ACTIVE lexical shape (Document → Chunk) with the source .msg/JSON pair referenced by path, never copied into the repo; and an email whose folder/process is NOT extractable is loadable WITHOUT an assignment, landing unassigned rather than being rejected or guessed. The assignment edge (email Document → ControlMFolder / process) is NEW relationship semantics and goes through the HITL gate: registered `status: planned` in relationship_vocabulary.yaml with a gate-prompt spec, and no loader writes it until the gate confirms. The SME assignment surface itself may be a later slice — this item's floor is "ingested, unassigned, and gate-registered".

Notes: User source 2026-07-26: "we get emails that reference a failure or activity and I've extracted those as json files and save the original msg format... it may require an SME assigning it to a particular folder or process if it's not extractable from the email." RETENTION, and it is the reason the source pair matters: Outlook purges at 6-18 months, but the extracts are deliberately preserved on a file server until the PROCESS OR PROJECT retires — so after ~18 months the file server holds the only copy and becomes a system of record with a backup obligation, not a cache. Purge is therefore project/process-scoped (a property-scoped delete), NOT a database drop — which is why retention did NOT end up being an argument for a separate database, contrary to the initial guess in that session. Same instinct as the "always keep the original requirement" rule. CLASSIFICATION: production-failure email almost certainly Internal- Confidential (real names, real folder names, incident detail). The .msg/JSON originals live OUTSIDE the repo — the gitignored-PDF precedent — and only mechanism-level references are committed. Distinct from the 2026-07-22 email-DL contact-point idea already in IDEAS: that one is about DL membership as an ontology mapping; this is the email BODY as a document corpus.

Q11Model document supersession and currency so 2-3 stale versions of the same internal process page are distinguishable by more than a date
ontologyP14requirementp3fable / ontology-mapper
docmeta
depends on: G31

Acceptance: A SUPERSEDES (or gate-chosen spelling) Document→Document relationship plus a currency signal on :Document is registered `status: planned` in relationship_vocabulary.yaml with a decision-matrix entry and a gate-prompt spec; a retrieval over a multi-version corpus can return ONLY the current version without relying on captured_at ordering alone. No loader writes the edge until the HITL gate confirms it.

Notes: User problem statement 2026-07-26: "internally i've seen 2 or 3 different versions with the same referenced person/team/process, but is stale and the only indication is date." The point that decided this is its own item rather than a rider on the database question: QUARANTINING DOES NOT DISAMBIGUATE. Putting all three versions in an unstructured-content database leaves all three versions sitting together, still ambiguous — the separation protects OTHER corpora from them, it does not tell you which one is current. That is a modeling problem and needs modeling, whatever the topology ends up being. Hence depends_on G31 only for where the nodes live, not for the semantics.

Q15Agent navigation QuerySpecs over the doc spine: name-addressable lookup, section browse, role siblings, search as FALLBACK
drydocs-apiP14taskp2sonnet / main
docmeta
depends on: Q13

Acceptance: Four registered QuerySpecs — docs.utility-lookup.v1 (exact name -> docs grouped by page_role), docs.section-browse.v1 (section -> siblings, one hop), docs.role-siblings.v1 (doc -> same-subject docs of another role), and docs.search.v1 (full-text/vector, explicitly the FALLBACK for "I don't know the name"). All four: bounded expansion with no unbounded variable-length traversal; every row carries source_url + doc_version + trust so the R5 citation surface renders provenance; chunk text is NOT returned by default — the agent asks for it as a deliberate second step. Proven against the loaded corpus, and reachable from /ask without touching /raw-cypher.

Notes: Section 4 of the plan. The point is that an agent usually arrives already knowing the noun ("how do I use exportdefjob?"), and a vector search for a known exact identifier is both slower and less reliable than an indexed exact match — so similarity search is demoted to the fallback path rather than being the only door in. Chunk-free by default because agent context is the scarce resource: triage on abstract + page_role, spend context on text only after choosing. Specs over raw Cypher per the R4/R5 design (ephemeral specs + citations carrying trust and classification) — this keeps doc answers citable and keeps agents off /raw-cypher.

Q16Documentation currency: surface the software-registry -> docs-corpus version drift as a report, and carry the pointer into the graph
drydocs-loadP14taskp2sonnet / main
docmeta
depends on: Q13

Acceptance: (a) A REPORT — its own verb or a section of Q7's docs-verify — prints one row per software-registry product carrying a `documentation:` block: product id, runtime `versions`, `docs_version`, and every runtime version NOT in `current_for`, with a status of current | drifted | unverified. Exits non-zero on drifted so a version bump cannot pass unnoticed in CI. (b) The pointer reaches the GRAPH so an agent can traverse from the product it is asked about to the documentation describing it, carrying docs_version + the currency status — via the term Q14's gate confirms (DESCRIBES is already registered for :Document -> :SoftwareProduct; this may need nothing new, which is the cheapest possible answer and should be checked FIRST). Nothing is written until that gate signs. (c) `current_for` is only ever set by a human confirming a capture against a runtime version — never derived by parsing version strings.

Notes: User requirement 2026-07-31: "we do need a pointer to it from the software registry which should contain external-vendor version number, so that when our ingestion process moves to the next major version, we know we need to update the docs." THE CONFIG HALF IS ALREADY BUILT (same session): the `controlm` product in software-registry.yaml carries `documentation: {corpus, docs_version, current_for}` beside its existing `versions:`, and two guards in test_software_registry.py enforce that the corpus id resolves to a real doc-source-registry entry and that the drift is COMPUTABLE (both sides present and typed). The mechanism is deliberately declaration-based: when the estate moves to a new version it lands in `versions:` and is absent from `current_for`, so the gap falls out with no version-string heuristics — because "does 9.0.20 documentation still describe 9.0.21.300?" is a judgement, not a string comparison. The drift-pinning guard asserts the KNOWN state rather than failing on it: drift is a true statement about the world today (9.0.20 docs, 9.0.21.300 runtime), not a code defect, so a red suite would be dishonest. What the test prevents is the drift changing SILENTLY. The operational alarm is this item's report. Home chosen beside `versions:` on the product rather than only on the doc corpus so the comparison is LOCAL — the two numbers a human must compare sit on adjacent lines.

R7Stale-source detection + rescrape queue: freshness fields on :Document, staleness check at answer time, queued full re-chunk refresh, rescrape-and-re-answer in the Ask spoke
drydocs-docmetaP15requirementp2sonnet / main
agentic-qa
depends on: R5 Q6

Acceptance: :Document nodes carry source_url, source_sha256, fetched_at, refresh policy, and a status lifecycle; answers flag citations whose source is stale per policy; rescrape is a QUEUED refresh artifact — auto-runnable for SYNTHESIZED corpora, re-queues HITL curation for CONFIRMED (the doc-source-registry sha256 rule, now code-enforced, never a silent overwrite); a changed sha triggers FULL re-chunk (a test proves position-resume is not used when content changed); "rescrape & re-answer" round-trips in the Ask spoke with both envelopes kept for comparison.

Notes: CLAIMED THEN RELEASED 2026-08-01 (desktop) — the claim is withdrawn, not abandoned mid-build: nothing was written, so the item is exactly as it was, plus this finding. A pre-build survey found a HARD dependency the item never recorded. "Rescrape is a QUEUED refresh artifact — AUTO-RUNNABLE for SYNTHESIZED corpora" needs something that can actually refetch, and the producer has NO fetcher: the web + filedrop connectors are Q6's deliverable ("Port A: bkup scrapers -> the producer docmeta component"), Q6 is todo and parked on physical media confirmed absent on both machines, and its web connector is additionally gated on Q12's page-count refusal. Building the queue anyway would ship a queue nothing can drain and an "auto-runnable" path that cannot run — worse than not building it, because the surface would claim a capability that does not exist. Q6 therefore added to depends_on. What is NOT blocked, and why a SPLIT is the obvious alternative for the user to rule rather than for an agent to assume: clauses (a) the :Document freshness fields + status lifecycle, (b) staleness flagging on citations, and (d) full re-chunk on a changed sha are all buildable today against the existing loaders — only the auto-run rescrape and the Ask spoke's round-trip need the fetcher. Splitting freshness-now from rescrape-later would unblock roughly three quarters of this item. One further gap the survey turned up, cheap to fix inside whichever shape wins: `agents/graph_qa/pipeline.py` hardcodes `"chunks": 0` in two places with the comment "doc-chunk retrieval arrives with R7 corpora wiring", so the agent currently has no Document-sourced SourceRecord at all. Clause (b) is still reachable without new retrieval architecture — the O18 docs QuerySpecs already return doc_ids, so a routed docs spec's rows can be joined to Document freshness and stamped onto the existing SourceRecord.fetched_at/stale slots — but the item's inputs name neither the pipeline nor the specs, so whoever builds it should widen them.

G32Rule the document/content database topology and restate the ddcontext charter: reference vs unstructured content vs derived findings
graph-infraP6taskp1fable / main
component-topology

Acceptance: One ruling through the HITL gate, recorded as an amendment to ADR 0002 and ADR 0006 (amendment notes, not body rewrites — the ADR 0002 banner precedent), fixing: (a) how many document/content databases exist and what each holds; (b) the ddcontext charter, which today says "isolated uncertain / SYNTHESIZED" and cannot honestly describe a VERBATIM-captured email — if unconfirmed content lands there the charter restates to UNCONFIRMED (authority not established, whether captured verbatim or inferred) and drydocs_api/exports.py's WATERMARK_VALUE follows, since it stamps "SYNTHESIZED — unverified" keyed on database name; (c) the target_db column of every doc-source-registry entry; (d) an explicit, recorded acceptance of what a boundary COSTS — see notes; (e) MERGED 2026-07-27: the drydocs-deepdoc CHARTER, which has drifted between ADR 0002 (reactive command-line dependency analysis — what the scaffold implements) and ADR 0006 §1 (a consumer of docmeta's corpus, citing Document/Chunk nodes) — rule whether deepdoc is a parser-driven investigator, a corpus-driven retriever, or both, against the user's stated intent: profile a Confluence space, then search it SEEDED from what the grounded graph already knows, creating no relationship unless the subject is already in the DryDocs graph (the ADR 0002 D1 proxy-node pattern, so the mechanism exists). 01_databases.cypher and provisioning/README.md then match the ruling, and tests/unit/test_database_names.py's read-vs-write guard covers the new set.

Notes: Design session 2026-07-26 (after G30). SME direction: THREE databases — structured / unstructured-content / derived — for separation of loads and wipe blast-radius during testing, both of which hold independently of retention. Retention turned out NOT to be an argument either way (see Q10). THE COST, to be accepted explicitly rather than discovered later. `ddall` federates reads but a relationship can NEVER span databases — a composite query is a join on returned values, not an edge walk (see the shape in provisioning/smoke_drydocs_all.cypher: chained `CALL { USE ddall.x }` blocks). So known-shape questions still work, restated as explicit joins, and every QuerySpec is fine. What is lost across the boundary: shortestPath and variable-length paths, all GDS (centrality, community, similarity), and a single vector index over reference AND content chunks — which becomes two index queries plus a client-side rerank, notable because the Essential GraphRAG corpus was ingested to do exactly that kind of retrieval. Also harder: text2cypher (Epic R / ADR 0007) must generate correct USE clauses and business-key joins rather than a plain MATCH. If those matter, the mitigation is a single-database analytical projection, and that should be decided here rather than improvised later. Blocks G31 (the spine cannot be sized until the database set is known) and through it Q9/Q10/Q11. Clause (e) merged at the 2026-07-27 groom from the 2026-07-26 deepdoc-scope inbox entry (user question; NOT covered by the database ruling alone). Rider: the graph-seeded-retrieval shape is the SECOND application of the same idea (first: the 2026-07-23 HR-hierarchy graph-seeded resolution) — name it as a reusable pattern in the ruling instead of re-deriving it. Until (e) is ruled, do not build the deepdoc scaffold against either stale charter.

G53Contingency plan: fold every database into one if multi-DB is ever unavailable
graph-infraP6taskp3opus / main
component-topology

Acceptance: A written consolidation plan (an ADR 0002 amendment or its own ADR) that answers each of these with a concrete mechanism rather than an intention: (1) HOW THE TRUST BOUNDARY SURVIVES — ADR 0002 D1 rejected the single-DB option because it commingles trust, so name the label namespace, the trust property, and the guard tests that would replace the transaction domain, and state plainly what protection is lost (the ADR's own words: multi-DB moves the guarantee "from discipline to physics", and one DB moves it back); (2) HOW ddschema's EXEMPLAR NODES COEXIST WITH drydocs' NODE KEYs — exemplars carry real labels WITHOUT the properties those keys require, which is the stated reason ddschema is its own database (G51), so this is a hard constraint conflict, not a naming collision; (3) WHAT REPLACES ddall — an enumeration of every QuerySpec, loader, and console route that names a non-default database or federates through the composite; (4) THE CONFIG CHANGE POINT — dev-environment.yaml's db-name map, and whether one map can serve both topologies so the fallback is a config switch rather than a code fork. Plan only: no provisioning, loader, or schema changes land from this item.

Notes: Worst-case contingency, groomed 2026-08-04 on the user's direction after the laptop's Neo4j Enterprise 30-day eval trial expired (2026-08-01). THE EXPIRY IS NOT THE TRIGGER and is explicitly out of scope — it is a local-testing license matter, the server keeps running, and writes were verified working after it (laptop, neo4jtest, drydocs DB: 2218 nodes, an Enterprise-only existence constraint created then dropped, six user databases online). What this plans for is Enterprise becoming genuinely unavailable — a company-side edition constraint or a cost ruling — because Community allows exactly ONE user database. That would collapse drydocs + ddlineage + ddcontext + ddschema into one store and remove the ddall composite entirely. ADR 0002 already priced this option and rejected it: "Community single DB + reliability property + :Candidate label namespace" is a listed D1 alternative, rejected because it commingles trust and "one bad query promotes noise". So this item does NOT re-open the decision — ADR 0002 stays accepted. It writes down the fallback we would otherwise have to improvise under duress, while there is time to think about it properly. The honest output may well be "consolidation costs us X and here is the sequence"; a plan that concludes the trust guarantee cannot be preserved in one database is a valid and useful result, provided it says so explicitly instead of papering over it.

J31The pull rule makes the CLAIM visible but not the WORK: from the other machine, a dead session and a working one look identical
docsP8chorep2sonnet / main
release-infrastructure

Acceptance: CLAUDE.md §0's pull rule gains a work-visibility clause carrying a MECHANISM rather than an intention, answering three things: (1) WHEN an in-flight session pushes work — a named trigger (first substantive edit, or the end of any session that did not close its item), not "when convenient"; (2) WHERE it goes — the `wip/<id>-<machine>` branch shape the K9 recovery already used, so the convention is discovered rather than invented; (3) WHAT a session must check before RELEASING someone else's claim back to todo — at minimum `git branch -r --list "wip/<id>-*"` — so a claim with work behind it cannot be declared dead. The clause cites both occurrences as its evidence and states plainly what it does NOT fix: a session that dies before its first push stays invisible, and no convention changes that.

Notes: Groomed 2026-08-04 (weekly groom) from the wip/k9-laptop inbox line, promoted SEPARATELY from the branch cleanup (J30) because the branch is a one-off and this is the rule. It EXTENDS a discipline the repo already ruled — J19 codified the pushed-claim rule after the 2026-07-28 C19 collision — rather than proposing a new direction, which is why it grooms as a chore instead of routing to the HITL gate. The exact wording stays the user's to confirm at build; what this groom fixes is that the gap now has an owner and an id. The cost is measured, not hypothetical: the laptop built a full K9 implementation and never pushed it, the desktop released the claim as dead (claim commit 3608ae5 was the tip and nothing followed) and rebuilt it, and main now carries 17d9e08 while bfb2f0b sits on a branch nobody planned. One push of a wip branch would have prevented the whole rebuild.

G54provision.ps1 cannot run as documented on a Docker-only host: it shells out to cypher-shell on the host PATH
graph-infraP6bugp2sonnet / main
component-topology

Acceptance: A host running the neo4jtest container with NO local Neo4j install can provision the topology by following the script's own header. Exactly one of two resolutions: (a) the script becomes exec-aware — detect that cypher-shell is absent from the host PATH and fall back to `docker cp` of the .cypher file plus `docker exec … cypher-shell -f`, reading the container name from config/dev-environment.yaml rather than hardcoding it; or (b) the header's REQUIRES block stops presenting host cypher-shell as satisfied by "bundled in the Neo4j Docker image" — it is bundled INSIDE the image, which is not the host PATH — and documents the docker-exec invocation as the supported Docker-host form. (a) is preferred, since the header already owns the `docker run` commands and therefore already assumes Docker on this path. Either way the fix respects and states the J29 trap: do NOT pipe file content into `docker exec`; copy the file in and use `-f`, because a PowerShell 5.1 pipe injects a UTF-8 BOM that cypher-shell then rejects.

Notes: Reported 2026-08-04 from the desktop M3 reload, where the documented invocation failed and the session worked around it with docker cp + docker exec cypher-shell -f. VERIFIED at the groom (laptop, static read — no database needed): the REQUIRES block at provision.ps1:6 says "cypher-shell on PATH (bundled in the Neo4j Docker image and tarball)" while the runner at :57 invokes bare `cypher-shell -a $Uri … -f`. On a Windows host whose only Neo4j is the container, that binary is not on PATH and the script cannot complete. The BOM half of the original inbox report was WRONG and is already closed: J29's byte-scan proved every tracked .cypher and .sql is clean, and the BOM came from the PS 5.1 pipe rather than from the repo. That is exactly why the trap belongs in this item's header text — the workaround shape and the encoding standard are one lesson, and J29's own close note says this gap "stays inboxed separately". p2 rather than p1: both machines already hold working topologies, so nothing is blocked today. What is broken is the documented path for a fresh host. WORKAROUND DOCUMENTED 2026-08-04 (does NOT close this item — the script itself is unchanged): the provisioning README now carries the explicit per-file docker cp + docker exec -f block, verified end to end (laptop, neo4jtest, 2026.05.0 EE — six cypher-shell calls exit 0, smoke federates 2218/0/46). It also records the J29 pipe trap with the MEASURED cause rather than the inferred one: PS 5.1's [Console]::OutputEncoding is UTF-8 WITH a 3-byte preamble that is written ahead of the first line on stdin redirection, and neither `Get-Content -Raw`, nor $OutputEncoding (already preamble-free), nor reassigning [Console]::OutputEncoding mid-session fixes it. That measurement belongs in this item's fix.

Q6Port A: bkup scrapers -> the producer docmeta component (cleaner/tokenizer/manifest/registry models; web + filedrop connectors; component boundary row)
drydocs-docmetaP14taskp2fable / main
docmeta
depends on: Q4 Q5

Acceptance: The docmeta component package exists under the name the Q4 gate decided (the modules-registry comment updated if it differs from the drydocs-docmeta working name): cleaner/tokenizer/manifest/registry-models ported from the bkup scrapers per the plan's §5 port table, with web + filedrop connectors; its own COMPONENT_GROUP in tests/unit/test_module_boundary.py + MODULE_MAP row; imports drydocs_core only (components never import each other); Track-1 portable tests cover parse/clean/hash offline (no network); boundary guard + full suite green. The web connector does NOT ship without Q12's page-count refusal — an unguarded bulk scraper is not an acceptable intermediate state (see Q12 for why: the trees in scope enumerate 2k-11k pages). CONNECTOR SHAPE (merged 2026-08-02, from the company-side answer to tracker T21): the two generic connectors are ACQUISITION ONLY — `fetch(source) -> list[RawPage]`, with cleaning and hashing downstream — over a `Connector` protocol plus a `RawPage` model and an unavailable-source error; `web` speaks public http(s) through stdlib urllib with an INJECTABLE TRANSPORT and an SSRF scheme allow-list; `filedrop` reads a file or a directory of md/txt/html through pathlib. The transport injection and the allow-list are NON-NEGOTIABLE, not preferences: the first is what makes the Track-1 offline tests real rather than network-dependent, and the second is the same guardrail Q12 exists to enforce. Reproduction is producer-authored against that protocol shape — never a copy of the other tree's files.

Notes: Groomed 2026-07-18 — docmeta plan P3. Module drydocs-docmeta registered at this groom as a WORKING NAME (the drydocs-api precedent) — final at the Q4 gate; this item renames the registry entry if the gate folds it into drydocs-deepdoc. Model fable per the component-boundary precedent (O5/G3). MERGED 2026-08-02 (weekly groom), from the [source->Q6] inbox line: the fetcher already exists company-side, and the split lands exactly where this item needs it — web and filedrop have ZERO internal dependencies, while the confluence connector and the separate scrapers CLI are internal-realm and stay there. Their own base.py designates web+filedrop as the "wired and offline-testable" generic pair, i.e. the boundary this port would have had to draw was drawn by the authors first, which is the strongest available signal that a reproduction is clean. Merged into the acceptance rather than opened as an item because it specifies work Q6 already owns. Unblocks three things at once: Q6 itself, R7 (released unbuilt 2026-08-01 for exactly this missing fetcher) and Q12 behind it. Direction rule stands — back-flow is SANITIZED REPRODUCTION, never a copy. . DONE 2026-08-04 (LAPTOP). PRODUCER-AUTHORED against the shape the T21 answer describes — the bkup repo is not mounted on this machine and the company tree was never read, so every line here was written from the plan's §5 table and the recorded connector contract. The T21 port row is discharged. . PATH: drydocs_docmeta/, NOT the plan's drydocs/docmeta/. That path predates the Phase B relocate; every component created since (remediation, lineage, deepdoc, api) is a top-level package while `drydocs` is the component REMAINDER. Recorded as an amendment note on ADR 0006 rather than a body edit (the ADR 0002 banner precedent) — the confirmed NAME and the separate-component ruling are untouched, only the physical path. . THE TWO NON-NEGOTIABLES ARE TESTED AS BEHAVIOUR, not as presence. The injectable transport is what makes that possible: `test_web_refuses_above_the_ ceiling_without_fetching_anything` asserts the TRANSPORT WAS NEVER CALLED, not merely that an exception was raised — a refusal that still fetched would pass the weaker test. Same for the SSRF allow-list: a run containing one file:// URL fetches NOTHING, including the good URLs before it, because a run that got 300 pages in before hitting it has already done the thing the list exists to stop. . Q12's clause is satisfied by construction rather than by a later item: the ceiling is enforced against the fully resolved location list BEFORE the first request, in the connector itself. See Q12 for the config half. . THREE DELIBERATE DEPARTURES from the plan's sketch, all recorded in the plan: (a) no T4 connector STUBS — a stub that raises is indistinguishable at a call site from a connector that is merely misconfigured, and this epic has spent three items on things that succeed loudly and do nothing; the producer ships the protocol and the company implements against it. (b) freshness.py is not a module yet: its primitive IS manifest.diff(), because the digest and the comparison over digests are one fact; what P5 adds is cadence and a queue. (c) The bkup curation vocabulary and the last_synced_from authority ladder live in registry.py as CODE vocabularies mapped onto the HITL gate positions, not as new ledger fields — they describe a DOCUMENT's state, and the ledger declares SOURCES. Adding columns to the YAML would have been a schema change nobody asked for. . NO GRAPH WRITE. Acquisition only; the load path is P4. The component writes nothing outside DRYDOCS_DATA_ROOT.

Q7Reconcile the doc-source registry against what is actually loaded: per-corpus declared / loaded / stale / missing status
drydocs-loadP14taskp2sonnet / main
docmeta

Acceptance: A `drydocs docs-verify` (name at implementer's discretion) reports one row per config/doc-source-registry.yaml entry: the declared target_db, whether that corpus is present in that database, its loaded Document/Chunk counts, and a status of loaded | missing | stale | wrong-db. `stale` = the registry's recorded sha256/captured_at no longer matches what the graph carries (the ADR 0006 §4 gate-preserving freshness rule); `wrong-db` = the corpus is present in a database OTHER than its declared target_db. Exits non-zero on any wrong-db row. Covers every corpus kind the registry admits — scraped web pages, filedrop PDFs, .msg/email extracts — because the check is registry- driven, not per-loader. A unit test drives it against a fake client.

Notes: DONE 2026-08-01 (laptop, concurrent with a desktop R-epic session — claimed in a module that session was not touching). `drydocs docs-verify` + drydocs/docs_verify.py (pure; `run(database, cypher, params)` is the only I/O seam) + 16 offline tests. Suite 1330. . THE DESIGN PROBLEM THE ITEM DID NOT KNOW ABOUT: the registry is corpus-keyed and THE GRAPH IS NOT. Only the Q13 vendor-docs loader writes `corpus_id`; bmc_docs identifies pages by repo-relative `path` and essential_graphrag writes one Document whose `doc_id` IS the corpus id. A "registry-driven, not per-loader" check (the acceptance's own words) is therefore impossible unless the registry says HOW to find each corpus — so every entry now carries `graph_locator: {match: corpus_id|doc_id|path_prefix|none, value: ...}`, guarded by a new test_doc_registry case. Teaching the verifier three loader shapes instead would have made it a per-loader registry that rots silently. . SIX STATUSES, NOT THE FOUR NAMED — two live situations cannot be described by loaded|missing|stale|wrong-db, and calling either "missing" would be a FALSE diagnosis: (a) `db-absent` — bmc-docs and bmc-controlm-utilities declare target_db `dddocs`, which schema/provisioning/01_databases.cypher HAS NEVER CREATED (it makes drydocs, ddlineage, ddcontext, ddall). That is not a load failure, it is G32's open topology ruling, so it reports and does not fail. (b) `unshaped` — jpmc-reports loaded as :DataAsset slices, never on the Document->Chunk spine; its documents are not missing, they were never documents. Only wrong-db exits non-zero, per the acceptance. . LIVE RUN — laptop, container `neo4jtest` (localhost:7687), 2026-08-01: 2 db-absent (both dddocs entries) · 1 loaded (essential-graphrag in ddcontext, 1 Document / 43 Chunks) · 2 unshaped. Exit 0. NOTE THE MACHINE: bmc-docs reports "not found in any existing database either" HERE, which is a statement about this laptop's graph, not about the corpus — the two machines hold separate graphs and git syncs neither. . Two accuracy defects in the first cut, both caught by reading the live output rather than the tests: `unshaped` said "no graph_locator declared" for entries that declared `match: none` on purpose (a ruling read as a silence), and `db-absent` short-circuited before probing, so it never reported WHERE the corpus actually is — the more useful half. Both fixed; the second is now the difference between "your target is unprovisioned" and "...and it is sitting in drydocs meanwhile". . Freshness is never silently reported as fresh: the older loaders write no captured_at, and the row says "uncheckable" rather than implying a comparison that did not happen. . User-requested 2026-07-26 ("we'll need to have a registry comparison or status to check if it's loaded into the db... scraped pages - pdf - etc"). The gap is real: doc-source-registry.yaml DECLARES corpora and test_doc_registry.py enforces the declaration's shape, but nothing anywhere checks that a declared corpus was ever actually loaded, or that it landed in the database it declared. The `wrong-db` case is the one worth the non-zero exit — it is the G30 failure class at corpus granularity. MODULE CHOICE: drydocs-load, not drydocs-docmeta, because the verb lives in cli.py beside m1-verify/m3-verify and must work BEFORE the docmeta component exists (Q6). Re-home it to docmeta if that component takes over corpus state. Startable now against the corpora that exist today (bmc-docs, essential- graphrag, jpmc-reports); the multi-DB cases arrive with G32.

Q8BUG: the bmc-docs DESCRIBES edge is silently dropped when SoftwareProduct is absent — a whole-corpus miss is indistinguishable from success
drydocs-loadP14bugp1sonnet / main
docmeta

Acceptance: A bmc-docs load in which ZERO :SoftwareProduct nodes are reachable fails loudly instead of reporting success with no DESCRIBES edges — either a hard error or a summary count the caller asserts on. Per-ROW misses (a single misspelled subject_product_id) must still be tolerated as they are today, so the fix distinguishes "this one row found no product" from "the product registry is not in this database at all". A test proves both halves: one row with a bad product id still loads its Document/Chunks and reports the miss; an empty-registry graph fails.

Notes: MEASURED 2026-07-26, drydocs/loaders/cypher/bmc_docs.cypher:97 — `OPTIONAL MATCH (sp:SoftwareProduct {product_id: row.subject_product_id})` guarded by `FOREACH (_ IN CASE WHEN sp IS NOT NULL ...)`. The template's own header already warns that a misspelled id silently drops the edge for that row; the same mechanism fires for an entire absent database and nobody noticed. Harmless TODAY (both the corpus and the software registry live in `drydocs`), which is exactly why it is worth fixing before anything moves: ADR 0006 already plans to re-target bmc-docs to `dddocs` at the P4 loader build while load-software-registry keeps writing `drydocs`, and a relationship cannot span databases — so that move would silently drop EVERY DESCRIBES edge and take the vendor→product routing spine with it. Third instance this session of "succeeds loudly, does nothing" (see G29, G30). Ready now and independent of the G32 topology ruling.

Q12Doc-capture scale guardrail: the web connector refuses a bulk scrape above a threshold instead of silently fetching thousands of vendor pages
drydocs-docmetaP14taskp2sonnet / main
docmeta
depends on: Q6

Acceptance: The web-capture entrypoint resolves its FULL target page list from the publisher's own manifest (e.g. the Author-it `toc.json`) BEFORE issuing any content request, and prints tree, page count, and estimated bytes + wall-clock at the configured delay. It REFUSES to proceed (non-zero exit, count named in the message) when the resolved count exceeds a configured threshold unless an explicit opt-in flag is passed; the threshold and the per-request delay are config values, never hardcoded literals. A `--dry-run` prints the same plan and exits 0 having fetched no content. Unit tests drive refusal-above and pass-below against a fixture manifest with NO network (the Track-1 portable rule). No capture script may land before this guard exists — Q6's acceptance carries the same clause.

Notes: User-requested 2026-07-31 after a live feasibility probe of the BMC help trees: "stop or warn the user if they try to scan that many with the script." The numbers are the argument, and they were measured, not estimated — documents.bmc.com publishes a machine-readable `toc.json` per tree, so the page count is knowable BEFORE fetching anything, which is exactly what makes a pre-flight refusal cheap and a post-hoc abort unnecessary: /supportu/INC/ (INCONTROL mainframe) = 10,912 pages ~210 MB raw; /supportu/9.0.20/ (distributed, complete) = 3,860 pages ~74 MB; /supportu/9.0.21/ (distributed, trimmed — no Utilities book) = 1,965 pages ~38 MB. Sampled pages averaged ~19 KB raw / ~6.9 KB extracted text. Two distinct harms the guard prevents, both real: hammering a third-party vendor site (robots.txt ALLOWS these paths, so politeness is entirely on us — there is no server-side backstop), and committing tens of MB of vendor copyright into a repo that is private-but-sometimes-published. A THIRD hazard motivated the request and belongs in the warning text: the operator can pick the wrong tree entirely and not notice. /supportu/INC/ is the INCONTROL MAINFRAME family (Control-D, Control-O, Control-M/Tape, JCL Verify) — its "Control-M" subtree at 40498.htm is Control-M **for z/OS** administration (CTMPLEX/Sysplex, started tasks, user exits, allocated datasets), NOT the distributed estate DryDocs models. So the pre-flight summary must name the TREE and its top-level books, not just a number — a count alone would not have caught this. . DONE 2026-08-04 (LAPTOP), and it closed in two halves rather than one build. THE REFUSAL ITSELF ALREADY EXISTED: scripts/external_vendor_scrape.py shipped it 2026-07-31 (e549f07) with --dry-run, the manifest-resolved page list, the book-by-book pre-flight naming the tree, and 14 offline tests — built ahead of this item's dependency because the capture needed it that day. What was NOT met was the acceptance's other clause, and it is the one that matters for a guardrail: "the threshold and the per-request delay are config values, never hardcoded literals". They were module constants (DEFAULT_MAX_PAGES = 600, DEFAULT_DELAY_SECONDS = 1.0). . WHY THAT CLAUSE IS NOT PEDANTRY, and the reason this closed WITH Q6 rather than before it: Q6 gave the repo a SECOND door to the same third party — the component's web connector. Two doors with two literals is two different notions of "too many pages", decided by which entrypoint the operator happened to use. So the numbers moved to config/doc-capture.yaml (schema drydocs.doc-capture.v1: max_pages, delay_seconds, timeout, retries, user_agent, and the SSRF scheme allow-list), both consumers read it, and a test asserts BOTH files reference CapturePolicy so a future third door cannot quietly re-hardcode. The refusal type moved with it — one rule, one exception class, raised from one place. . The scraper's own fetch() now also enforces the scheme allow-list, which it never did: it would have followed a file:// URL in a tree definition. . Registered as an enforcement-matrix surface (config/doc-capture.yaml -> scripts/external_vendor_scrape.py + drydocs_docmeta/connectors/web.py), so the config-surface guard covers it like every other governed config file.

Q13STANDALONE repeatable vendor-docs ingestion + loader: capture -> convert -> load a NAVIGABLE spine (TOC hierarchy + page_role + abstract), taxonomy only
drydocs-loadP14taskp2sonnet / main
docmeta

Acceptance: A STANDALONE, REPEATABLE pipeline — its own converter, loader, cypher and `drydocs` verb, NOT a widening of load-bmc-docs (user ruling 2026-07-31: "unique enough to use a stand alone, but repeatable ingestion and loader"). It reads the capture manifest as its contract, so capture / convert / load are three re-runnable stages and re-running any of them is idempotent (MERGE on doc_id; a second full run reports zero net change). The converter strips the Author-it/ExtJS navigation chrome and emits body markdown; the loader writes :Document with doc_id, title, breadcrumb, source_url, sha256, captured_at, a deterministic `abstract` (first paragraph) and a derived `page_role`; the toc.json hierarchy loads as IN_SECTION / SUBSECTION_OF; chunks keep the established (:Chunk)-[:PART_OF]->(:Document) + NEXT_CHUNK shape. A TOC node bearing a fragment (`page` + `anchor` in the manifest) attaches to the EXISTING document as a section — it must NOT mint a second :Document. `page_role` derivation is an explicit title rule, NEVER an LLM, and the run REPORTS its unclassified count rather than defaulting pages silently (the G16 values-decide pattern). Every node carries doc_version and version_verified=false. NO meaning edges — no :ControlMUtility, no DOCUMENTS, no SEE_ALSO (Q14's gate). Counts reported per role.

Notes: Layer 1 of knowledge/upgrade-plans/vendor-docs-agent-navigation.md, and the half that needs NO ruling because it only transcribes the publisher's own structure — the taxonomy-before-ontology rule in CLAUDE.md §1. Worth doing alone: hierarchy + role + abstract already beats similarity-search-only, which is all the corpus supports today. STANDALONE, decided 2026-07-31: load-bmc-docs cannot be stretched to cover this and should not be. It consumes hand-written markdown, splits on H2, and infers a provenance TIER from heading text — a heuristic that exists only because that corpus is our lossy paraphrase. This corpus is VERBATIM throughout, so there is no tier to infer, and its real structure (TOC hierarchy, page_role, anchors) has nowhere to go in that loader's model. Two loaders with honest contracts beat one with a mode flag. REPEATABLE means the capture manifest is the contract between stages: scripts/external_vendor_scrape.py already emits it (sha256 per document, page/anchor per TOC node), so a recapture after a version bump re-runs the same convert+load with no code change. MODULE: drydocs-load beside bmc_docs.py, not drydocs-docmeta — the Q7 precedent (it must work BEFORE the docmeta component exists at Q6). The version properties are load-bearing, not bookkeeping: the capture is 9.0.20 (BMC ships NO Utilities book in the trimmed 9.0.21 publication) while the estate runs 9.0.21.300, so the graph must make "the 9.0.20 docs say X, unverified for your estate" the only answer it can produce. The config-side half of that already exists — see Q16. . DONE 2026-08-04 (LAPTOP). The pipeline itself landed 2026-07-31 in 9713118 — converter, loader, cypher, both CLI verbs, 41 offline tests — and the status was never flipped, so the repo went on advertising Q13 in next_ready for four days. This close is the VERIFICATION that flip should have carried, and it found three defects. None of them would fail a test or a load; all three are the "succeeds loudly, does nothing" reporting class this epic hit at G29, G30 and Q8. . (1) THE LOADER COULD NOT REPORT A CHANGE. vendor_docs.cypher had no WAS_GENERATED_BY provenance tail, and BaseLoader derives rows_changed by counting the edges a run attached — so it reported rows_changed=0 on a FIRST load exactly as on a no-op re-run, which makes this item's own idempotence evidence ("a second full run reports zero net change") unfalsifiable. `to_params` had been computing row_checksum all along and nothing read it. Fixed with the delta-only tail (bmc_docs / controlm_folders idiom), placed ABOVE the SUBSECTION_OF block because that block's UNWIND of an empty list drops every row at TOC depth <= 1 from the rest of the statement — the tail-ordering trap bmc_docs.cypher documents, and a test now pins the ordering. . (2) A CAPTURE IS NOT A CORPUS, and the loader wrote the wrong one. It set corpus_id to the CAPTURE id (bmc-controlm-9.0.20-utilities) while this item's own input, config/doc-source-registry.yaml, declares `graph_locator: {match: corpus_id, value: bmc-controlm-utilities}` — so Q7's `docs-verify` would have reported a fully loaded corpus as MISSING. A false negative in the one check built to stop false claims. It survived because nothing has ever run both halves together. Now every row carries BOTH: corpus_id resolved REGISTRY-DRIVEN (the entry naming this capture in its `manifest:` path — never a string transform of the capture id, and an unregistered capture refuses per docmeta invariant 1), and capture_id for which fetch produced the row. A test asserts the loaded id equals what the registry's locator searches for, so the two can no longer drift apart. . (3) doc_id WAS THE BARE TOPIC STEM ("16200"), globally unique across every capture. Author-it reuses topic ids across publications, so loading the already-registered 9.0.21 Parameters tree would have MERGEd onto the 9.0.20 Utilities documents and overwritten doc_version — silently destroying the one distinction this corpus exists to carry. doc_id and section_id are now capture-scoped. "MERGE on doc_id" stays literally true; it is doc_id that got an honest identity. Free to change because nothing is loaded anywhere yet — the build-order argument G31 makes. . Also closed the literal acceptance gap: doc_version + version_verified now ride :Chunk and :DocSection, not only :Document, so a chunk that surfaces alone in a retrieval result still carries the version caveat (Q15 returns chunk-grain rows; this is what lets those rows cite honestly). . NO LIVE RUN — ON EITHER MACHINE, and this is the honest state rather than an untested one (J18). Two independent blocks: the registry entry is `confirmed: false`, so `_gate_loader` refuses the write until an SME gate signs the doc-graph shape; and the 1016-page capture lives in the DESKTOP's data root (DRYDOCS_DATA_ROOT is machine-local and git syncs none of it), so this laptop has no payload to convert. Evidence here is offline and reproducible anywhere: 60 tests over a fixture capture, including two full loads through a recording client proving the checksums are identical across runs — which is precisely the condition under which the new delta-only tail fires zero times. The graph-side count stays unproven until a machine holding the capture runs it post-gate. . Registry entries for the two remaining trees: the 9.0.21 Parameters tree in scripts/external_vendor_scrape.py has NO doc-source-registry entry, so convert refuses it by design rather than inventing a corpus id.

G13's owning-job resolution activates on the feed: a unit test proves a job whose CMD_LINE moves a file plans job->asset edges in plan_curated output. Notes: Groomed 2026-07-19 from two G13-subagent inbox findings: the missing-feed line (only INVOKES/TRIGGERS emitted today, so G13's resolution logic is built + tested but dormant) promoted as this item; the sibling surface-unresolved_file_ops line FOLDED into this acceptance rather than itemized separately — the feed is what makes the counter worth reading. O27QuerySpec authoring conventions: consume gated edges not raw staging columns; canonical external ref grammar; no graph element ids on external surfaces
drydocs-apiP12chorep3sonnet / main
web-console

Acceptance: A conventions section (drydocs_api/ docstring or a sibling doc, discoverable from query_specs.py) records three rules with rationale: (a) specs read gate-confirmed materialized edges and properties, never re-derive meaning from raw staged columns (precedence resolution lives in the loaders — the Backstage consume-the-relation-not-the-spec-field rule); (b) a canonical external ref grammar kind:namespace/name (namespace = data center where names collide across the 4 DCs) is defined for deep links and export manifests; (c) graph-internal element ids / uids never appear in URLs, manifests, or exports (documented as unstable). Existing registry specs are audited against (a) with violations fixed or flagged; a registry invariant or unit test enforces (c) where mechanical.

Notes: Groomed 2026-07-22 from assessment T2+T3 (one conventions home: two consumption rules + the ref grammar). p3 — largely codifies what O11 already does. CLOSED 2026-08-01. drydocs_api/AUTHORING.md holds the three rules with rationale, linked from query_specs.py's module docstring. The audit found the registry ALREADY compliant on all three — which was the point worth writing down: compliance was a property of careful authoring, not of the system, so nothing would have caught the first violation. Three things the work changed relative to how the item was framed: (1) The ref-grammar namespace is NOT the data center. O27 was groomed "namespace = data center where names collide across the 4 DCs"; checked against drydocs_core/schema/constraints.cypher that holds for exactly ONE node kind (ControlMHostGroup's (data_center, name) NODE KEY). ControlMJob and Condition — the two kinds most likely to appear in a deep link — are FOLDER-scoped ((folder_id, job_id) and (folder_id, name)). The rule shipped as "namespace = the declared uniqueness scope", which yields the data center where the key says so and the folder where it does not, and self-maintains because a new loader's NODE KEY already states its namespace. The doc's table is held to the constraint file by test_authoring_doc_namespace_table_matches_declared_keys — otherwise it would be a record checkable only against itself. (2) Rule (a) needed its NON-violations stated, or it would push authors into worse Cypher. explorer.conditions.v2's property join looks like a violation and is not: folder_id is a declared NODE KEY component, so the join resolves identity rather than re-deriving a relationship. Flagged not fixed — no folder->condition edge exists (conditions attach to JOBS via REQUIRES_IN_CONDITION/EMITS_OUT_CONDITION), and materialising one is an ontology decision for the gate, never a read-spec edit. (3) The element-id guard went where the RISK is, not where the item pointed. The acceptance said "a registry invariant"; the registry is hand-authored and reviewed and had zero violations, while the ephemeral path (R4) carries LLM-authored Cypher into the same export/manifest code. Guarding only the registry would have put the check on the path that cannot fail. Both paths now call one guard.ensure_no_element_ids — placed in guard.py beside ensure_read_only, which also earns it _code_regions' comment/string stripping (RETURN 'id(' as data cannot false-positive; the ADR 0003 lesson) and its lower-casing, which is what makes the check case-insensitive across ElementId( / ID( spellings. The text2cypher prompt carries the rule too: the prompt prevents, the guard catches. Zero first-party elementId uses exist repo-wide, so all of it is regression-only. Suite 1277 passed / 5 skipped (was 1272); ruff check + format clean.

O28Node status envelope for the inspector + hub health glyphs: per-source namespaced status items, derived never hand-authored (status.items pattern)
drydocs-webP12requirementp3sonnet / main
web-console

Acceptance: A documented node-status envelope — a list of per-source items {type (source-namespaced), level (info|warning|error), message, error?}, always DERIVED by a producing system, never hand-authored — is adopted as the contract for the inspector sidebar's status section and the landing hub's spoke health glyphs; at least ONE real producer is wired end-to-end (first candidate: the loader per-run WARN/reject stream attaching to its :JobRun / loaded nodes) through a QuerySpec returning that shape; the envelope doc records the Backstage status.items precedent and the rule that new sources add namespaced types, never new shapes.

Notes: Groomed 2026-07-22 from assessment T5. Requirement (future capability) — the layer-4 context-graph surface in the UI; p3 until a module needs live health. CLOSED 2026-08-01. Contract in knowledge/standards/node-status-envelope.md (Backstage status.items precedent recorded, plus the two rules: always DERIVED by a producing system, and new sources add NAMESPACED TYPES never new shapes). Producer = BaseLoader._close_run via status_items_for(), a PURE function of the run summary — which is what makes "always derived" a testable property rather than a promise (8 tests, no database). Wire = loads.status-items.v1, live end-to-end as the /loads Status frame: loader derives -> rides the :JobRun -> spec unwinds -> SpecGrid renders. UI adoption via one shared component (StatusItems + HealthGlyph) used by BOTH the Explorer inspector's status section and the landing hub's spoke glyphs, so a new producer lights both with no UI change. Four design decisions worth keeping: (1) A PROPERTY on :JobRun, not a node + relationship. A relationship type is an ontology decision that goes through the HITL gate (CLAUDE.md §1), and a derived health signal has no ontological meaning to gate — modelling it as an edge would have made every future producer's rollout gate-bound for no semantic gain. This is why O28 could close without a gate session. (2) JSON strings, not parallel lists. Neo4j cannot hold a map inside a list property; parallel lists (status_types/status_levels/...) would put the envelope's integrity in the ALIGNMENT of three lists, where one mismatched append silently mislabels an item's severity. One string per item cannot desynchronise. (3) NO all-clear item. A clean run stores an empty list, so "healthy" (a producer ran, nothing to report) stays distinguishable from "unknown" (no :JobRun at all). Collapsing them is how a dashboard ends up green because nothing is watching — the hub renders every spoke except loads as UNKNOWN today, deliberately, rather than a false green. (4) The demo inspector is fed through the SAME parser as the live path (demoStatus.ts authors JSON strings, parseStatusItems reads them), so a shape change breaks the demo surface loudly instead of leaving it rendering a format nothing produces. Scope note: the Explorer inspector's items are demo-derived because that canvas is the SYNTHESIZED demo graph — wiring it to live data needs a demo->live selection bridge that does not exist. That is graph plumbing, not contract work; the acceptance's "at least ONE real producer wired end-to-end" is met by the /loads Status frame, which is fully live. The O42 component-ledger guard fired on the new component and was answered rather than silenced: StatusItems is registered UNBOUND (26/63) because it serves two modules, so binding it to either would misstate it. Suite 1285 passed / 5 skipped; ruff clean; tsc + vite build clean.

R6Tier-2 bounded graph-of-thoughts loop: enhance/solve controller with hard caps and the task-scoped context graph per the R1 residency ruling

depends on: R2 R3

Acceptance: The loop engages only when Tier-1 context is insufficient; hard caps enforced (iterations <=2, next-step vote x3, fix loop <=2, per-question token budget) with a forced-solve fallback so every run terminates with an answer or an explicit "cannot answer"; the enhance branch writes ONLY the task-scoped context graph named by the R1 ruling (never drydocs / ddlineage — a test proves it); per-iteration snapshots are captured and renderable in the existing d3 graph pane; the R3 ledger records per-iteration cost so caps are tunable from data.

Notes: CLOSED 2026-08-01. tier2.py is a PURE controller taking llm/retrieve callables rather than the pipeline, so the whole loop is testable with fakes and the private coupling stays in pipeline.py; retrieval REUSES Tier 1, which is why the <=2 fix loop and the read-only pre-flight are inherited rather than reimplemented — there is exactly one path that runs Cypher, and a test pins the executed sequence. Two bounding decisions the acceptance did not settle, both stated in the module docstring: (1) a tie or unparseable vote counts as SOLVE — the failure mode of voting is not a wrong answer, it is a loop that will not stop, so ambiguity resolves toward terminating; (2) the token budget bounds EXPLORATION, not the whole run — once spent the loop stops enhancing, but evidence already bought still gets one final answer call, deliberately over budget and recorded as budget.exhausted. Refusing that last call would spend the budget gathering evidence and then discard it unanswered. With NO evidence and no budget the run makes zero calls. The R1 residency proof is STRUCTURAL, not behavioural: task_graph.py has no driver, session, database name or Cypher in it, and the test reads the source and fails if any appears. A comment saying "do not persist this" is not a control; a module with nothing to persist with is. Also fixed a real gap the acceptance named only in passing — the ledger recorded iteration=1 for EVERY call, so per-iteration cost was unavailable and the caps were untunable from data. The loop now drives the iteration counter and a test pins round 2's calls to round 2. Every cap reports whether it BIT (forced_solve, budget.exhausted), and the budget is reported on runs it never touched — a limit you can only see once it has bitten is one nobody can tune before it does. Web: rather than a second d3 copy, the deterministic placement engine moved to lib/forceLayout.ts and GraphExplorer now consumes it; the new ask/TaskGraphPane keeps its OWN visual language on purpose, because a viewer who mistakes a synthesized sub-question for a real graph node has been actively misled. Snapshots are cumulative per phase (start / iteration N / final) so any frame renders alone. Suite 1315; tsc + build clean; O42 ledger 27/64 (TaskGraphPane bound to ask/).

Notes: Split out of G28 at its close (2026-07-26) — G28 bundled this with the naming cleanup and they are different kinds of decision: G28 was mechanical drift against an already-ruled convention, this one changes WHERE curated lineage data lives. Measured: drydocs_lineage/writer.py line 70 pins DATABASE = "drydocs" and refuses any other database (TrustBoundaryError, an allowlist — so this is a correctness and emptiness problem, not a safety one); drydocs_api/query_specs.py declares database="ddlineage" on four specs (lines 417, 540, 565, 589). The ruling is written up in ADR 0002 as a "Residency clarification" — NOT an amendment, because D1/D2 already say drydocs-lineage writes ground truth to `drydocs` and were never amended when G1 provisioning added a fourth database two days later. Each half of the codebase had followed a different source and both cited one, which is why neither looked wrong alone. Three grounds: (1) the stated reason for the DB boundary is the TRUST axis, and curated lineage is VERBATIM/GROUNDED — the same tier as the main load, so splitting it serves nothing; (2) 0002-C §5 already carries a ticked, tested "writes ground truth only" gate; (3) decisive — write_curated MATCHes :ControlMJob nodes the M3 load owns in `drydocs` and deliberately never MERGEs them (a lineage-created stub would break m3-verify's "every job has a folder"). A transaction cannot span databases, so from ddlineage those MATCHes would match nothing and every job-endpoint edge would vanish SILENTLY — an unmatched MATCH yields zero rows, not an error. Option (a) is therefore a design (a :ControlMJob proxy spine in ddlineage), not a constant flip. Deferring is also the cheap direction: ddlineage is empty, so choosing it later costs a design, not a migration. Named trigger to revisit, as an ADR amendment through the gate: the docmeta gate (ADR 0006 §b) adopted `dddocs` on the same component-per-database pattern and re-targets the bmc-docs corpus out of `drydocs` — if that proves out and lineage grows its own proxy spine, reopen. Verified load-bearing: restoring one spec to ddlineage fails 3 tests. Also corrected the user-visible UI copy that named ddlineage (module registry `backsOnto`, the Lineage SYNTHESIZED banner, route comments) — the honest reason those frames are empty is the vocabulary gate, not the database. Suite 928 -> 930.

G33Load the DryDocs code-dependency snapshot into the graph under a Project root, using the seeded-but-unused software ontology
drydocs-loadP16requirementp2fable / main
self-documentation

Acceptance: A `drydocs load-code-snapshot` verb reads the newest knowledge/depgraph-snapshots/*.json (schema depgraph-machine-first/v1) and MERGEs it idempotently into `drydocs`, so it survives the wipe-and-rebuild cycle like every other loader and is re-runnable from committed files with zero data loss (the ADR 0002 D3 stateless test). Everything hangs off an explicit (:Project {project_id:'drydocs'}) root so "is this our own code?" is one hop, and modules carry a label that CANNOT collide with the software registry's :SoftwareProduct nodes. The new node classes and relationships plus their binding to the SWO terms are registered `status: planned` and go through the HITL gate; no loader writes them until it confirms. snapshot.ps1 stays DECOUPLED — the session ritual must keep working with no database running. GATE SIGNED OFF 2026-07-27 (config/gate-prompts/self-documentation-code-graph.yaml, 36/36, 0 edits) — THIS ITEM IS NOW CLEARED TO BUILD and the acceptance is tightened to what was ruled. FIVE registrations already exist at `status: planned`, not the four this text originally named: node classes :Project and :CodeModule, edges HAS_MODULE, IMPORTS and IS_ENCODED_IN (the third added by the §E1(b) SWO ruling). Build to these rulings: (1) ONE :Project root; the snapshot's SIX scan roots are a `project` PROPERTY on :CodeModule, never sibling nodes (§B1a). (2) :CodeModule is keyed on `file_id`, the repo-relative path — the only non-colliding key (§C1a/§C4). (3) `circular` loads as a property (§C3). (4) edges carry the M2 pull-provenance fields first_seen_at / last_seen_at / last_run_id like every other loader (§D3). (5) IS_ENCODED_IN targets the already-seeded SwoClass Python, value derived from node.extension (§E1b). (6) the :CodeModule / :SoftwareProduct exclusion is a GRAPH-TEST, not a constraint — Neo4j cannot declare label mutual-exclusion (§F1). (7) the loader DISCRIMINATES ON meta.tree and REFUSES tree-mode input (§G1a) — BUILD NOTE 2026-07-27, post-sign-off review finding: the tree-mode files carry NO `meta` key AT ALL (verified: top-level keys are schema/projects/nodes/edges/stats/rels), so there is no `tree: true` to refuse on — and a naive `*.json` name-sort picks tree-this-version.json as "newest" ('t' > 'd'). A literal negative check (`meta.get('tree')` truthy → refuse) would therefore ACCEPT the tree files. Implement as a POSITIVE assertion: select `drydocs-*.json` only, require `meta` PRESENT and `meta.tree == false`, refuse loudly on anything else. The §G1(a) ruling stands; this fixes only its literal reading. SECOND selection subtlety, found LIVE at the first build run: a date-only name (drydocs-20260727.json, a one-off) sorts ordinally AFTER every timed name of the same day ('.' > '-'), so raw string sort picked the stale 12:42 capture over the 17:33 one. "Newest by filename timestamp" (§H2) must PARSE the drydocs-YYYYMMDD[-HHMM] timestamp, date-only keyed as 0000. (8) `abs_path` is DROPPED; load rel_path / file_id only (§H4). (9) a refusal must be LOUD — see the "succeeds loudly, does nothing" family. (10) register the source in config/source-registry.yaml with classification Internal-Public (§H6).

Notes: User source 2026-07-26: load the DryDocs project code snapshot under the software ontology, "which may prevent missing code fixes", plus "a way to distinguish it's for drydocs project and not the products metadata". Cheap because both halves already exist and neither is used: ontology.cypher seeds 7 SwoClass + 6 SwoProperty terms (Python, Shell, SQL, "is encoded in", "implements", "has specified data input/output", "uses platform") with ZERO consumers anywhere in the tree, and snapshot.ps1 already writes a code dependency graph every session that never enters Neo4j — it is JSON plus a viewer.html. *** THREE PREMISES IN THIS NOTE WERE WRONG AND ARE CORRECTED HERE (gate self-documentation-code-graph, 2026-07-27). All three came from describing the artifact without opening it, and finding them was the gate's main value: (i) it said the depgraph schema "already carries a `projects` key (=1), so the Project root matches the producer's own shape rather than inventing one". That figure belongs to tree-this-version.json, a one-off TREE-mode capture (540 files + 124 dirs, projects: ['drydocs1'], a CONTAINS relationship). The rolling dependency snapshots are 183 files / 98 edges — the real load is about a QUARTER the size this note implied. (iii) both shapes declare the SAME schema string depgraph-machine-first/v1, so a loader keying on it alone mis-reads one; §G1 ruled discrimination on meta.tree. *** TARGET IS `drydocs`, NOT dddocs, and the stated purpose is what decides it: "prevent missing code fixes" means traversing from a DryDocs module to the loader to the Control-M job it loads. That is a path query, so they must share a database — and code is not reference material ABOUT a system, it IS one, deterministically derived from source we control with no authority ambiguity. Same character as folders and jobs. The Project root is what keeps it from polluting product metadata. RE-PHASED 2026-07-26, same day, on the user's call. Notes: Groomed 2026-08-02 from the 2026-08-01 [chore] line. The instance: test_schema.py::test_constraint_count counted `CREATE CONSTRAINT` ANYWHERE in the file, so documenting a DDL trap in a `//` comment inflated the count and simultaneously false-failed test_constraints_are_idempotent (the comment "has no IF NOT EXISTS"). S3 fixed it by anchoring both at line start, mirroring constraints.py::_DECLARATION_RE, which the bootstrap presence guard already used. The generalisation is the point: a guard that reads prose makes the file unable to explain itself, and this repo's whole doctrine is that the reasoning IS the audit trail. SECOND INSTANCE (merged 2026-08-04 from the G51-tail inbox line): the same day produced a sibling of the same family — test_databases_match_provisioning_script promised "exactly what 01_databases.cypher creates" in its docstring and asserted only yaml ⊆ cypher, so a database added to the DDL and not the config passed green. Made bidirectional at the G51 tail (aa0a0eb) and it FAILED on ['ddschema'] before the config fix — the audit proven, not argued. The sweep's inventory should cover promise-vs-assertion mismatches (docstring says equality, code checks subset), not only substring anchoring. J28 is the third family member (a classifier, kept separate as a bug with a proof case).
